# pycc Design Decisions (ADR log)

Format: one entry per irreversible-ish call. Statuses: `proposed` → `accepted` → (`superseded by D-xxx`). Changing an accepted decision requires a new entry, not an edit.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D-001 | `int` = hybrid: `i64` fast path, overflow promotes to heap bigint. CPython semantics preserved; `--int native` opt-in deviation for max speed | accepted |
| D-002 | Backend: LLVM (inkwell) as the only v1 backend. Cranelift for debug builds = post-1.0 experiment | proposed |
| D-003 | Parser: vendor `ruff_python_parser` for v0.1–0.5 velocity; replace with own parser in v0.6 (grammar-coverage gate proves parity) | proposed |
| D-004 | Memory: RC + inferred ownership (moves, borrows, elision) + Bacon-Rajan cycle collector for the residue. No tracing GC, ever | proposed |
| D-005 | Exceptions: native unwinding (Itanium/SEH), zero-cost happy path — not result-codes | proposed |
| D-006 | Generics: monomorphization; vtable dispatch only for explicit dynamic-Protocol use and `--opt-size` cold code | proposed |
| D-007 | `str` = UTF-8 (not PEP 393 UTF-32 arrays). Codepoint indexing via lazy offset index. Rationale: memory, SIMD, FFI; deviation invisible except perf profile | accepted |
| D-008 | No pycc-wide GIL in native execution; thread safety = compile-time Shareable/move checks (Rust Send/Sync analog), with an embedded CPython boundary retaining its own local GIL under D-128 | proposed |
| D-009 | Stdlib written in typed Python, compiled by pycc itself; Rust intrinsics only at the syscall/math floor | proposed |
| D-010 | Diagnostics: codes stable forever, JSON format versioned, `explain` registry mandatory per code | proposed |
| D-011 | Cross-platform is Tier-1 from v0.1: Windows/MSVC is CI-gated day one, bundled lld, no system toolchain required | proposed |
| D-012 | Language level: exactly CPython 3.14 in v1.0 (`python = "3.14"` in pycc.toml). No per-version grammar switches before v1.x; a later standard Python level requires a superseding ADR plus a machine-checked support/cumulative-fixture/oracle mapping. pycc-specific syntax remains forbidden | proposed |
| D-013 | Development model: AI-first — the compiler is written by AI agents; these specs are the executable contract. Every spec claim must be mechanically checkable (test, benchmark gate, or CI rule), because "the spec" is what agents optimize against | proposed |
| D-014 | Testing: 100% line+region coverage (`cargo llvm-cov`), CI-gated on every PR from v0.1 on. Exemptions are whole-file only (`--ignore-filename-regex`), each entry justified in TESTING.md — no function-level opt-out exists on stable Rust | accepted |
| D-015 | Codegen toolchain pin: LLVM 22.1.1 (Homebrew, single keg — the `llvm@17`..`llvm@22` opt-paths on the dev host are stale symlinks to it, not distinct installs) + `inkwell = "0.9"` with feature `llvm22-1` | accepted |
| D-016 | Vendored parser pin (D-003): `ruff_python_parser = "0.0.6"`, `ruff_python_ast = "0.0.6"` (crates.io, checked at PR-1 time — re-verify before bumping) | accepted |
| D-017 | No separate `pycc_lexer` crate for v0.1: the vendored `ruff_python_parser` bundles lexing internally and nothing in the v0.1 pipeline consumes a standalone token stream. `pycc_lexer` is created when D-003's own-parser work (v0.6) actually needs to expose one | accepted |
| D-018 | `pycc_testkit` deferred past PR-1/PR-2: no PEP conformance matrix exists yet for a real harness to check against; `tests/slice0.rs` covers the two named fixtures ad hoc in the meantime, built for real at PR-4/PR-6 | proposed |
| D-019 | Agent surfaces: repository-owned agents and skills support both OpenAI Codex and Claude Code; `AGENTS.md` is the shared instruction source and `CLAUDE.md` imports it | accepted |
| D-020 | CI trust: pull-request code is untrusted; workflow permissions default to explicit read-only/none, and workflow changes are audited from the trusted base revision | accepted |
| D-021 | Every agent task starts from refreshed repository/spec/API evidence and never mutates a stale or dirty base implicitly | accepted |
| D-022 | Confirmed iEvo defects may be reported publicly without per-report permission, within strict scope and privacy limits (privacy clause narrowed by D-087) | accepted (privacy clause superseded by D-087) |
| D-023 | Auto-evolution intent is shared, but executable hooks and generated scripts remain machine-local | accepted |
| D-024 | `main` is protected by PR, CI, resolved conversations, and an audited emergency path; independent approval starts when a second maintainer is available | accepted ("not delegated to routine tasks" clause narrowed by D-125) |
| D-025 | Shared hook targets require a registered, CI-discovered fail-silent clean-clone contract | accepted |
| D-026 | PR-3's cross-compilation proof is same-OS/cross-arch (macOS arm64 ↔ x64 via `pycc build --target`), not cross-OS: cross-OS needs a real target sysroot, not just a bundled linker, verified empirically. Cross-OS compilation remains a tracked future gap, not silently dropped | accepted |
| D-027 | Windows linking against the official prebuilt LLVM 22 release additionally needs a real `libxml2` (installed via vcpkg, `x64-windows-static-md` triplet, aliased to `xml2s.lib`) — that release's own `llvm-config` reports it as a system lib, so `llvm-sys` requires it regardless of which LLVM components pycc_codegen actually calls | accepted |
| D-028 | Windows has no system `cc`, and clang's bare-invocation default target proved unreliable — every place `pycc` invokes a linker directly (both `src/main.rs` and, independently, `pycc_codegen`'s own test helper) needs the bundled `clang`, an explicit `-target`, and an explicit Windows system-libs list | accepted |
| D-029 | LLVM's `LLVMDisposeMessage` crashes (access violation) on Windows for the official prebuilt 22.1.1 release — hit at `module.verify()`, `TargetTriple`'s `Drop`, and two `LLVMString`-returning error paths; each needed its own targeted fix | accepted |
| D-030 | CI steps must not force `shell: bash` on Windows without a reason — Git Bash's own `PATH` reordering can shadow MSVC's linker with its bundled coreutils `link.exe`, breaking unrelated builds | accepted |
| D-031 | Linux's default `cc` (GCC) rejects clang-only `-target` syntax — `pycc`'s linker step routes through the bundled clang on Linux whenever `--target` is given, leaving macOS/Windows unchanged | accepted |
| D-032 | Branch protection requires a single `ci-gate` job that fans in every `ci.yml` job, not per-matrix-leg required checks — matrix-leg names bake in matrix values and go stale if the matrix ever changes | accepted |
| D-033 | Branch protection's required-checks switch to `ci-gate` (proposed by D-032) is live on `main` — recorded as a new entry rather than an edit to D-024/D-032 | accepted |
| D-034 | Checked roadmap claims are release policy and are validated against pull-request data by a checker executed from the trusted base revision | accepted |
| D-035 | PR-4 is frontend-only: `pycc_mir`/`pycc_codegen` gain no new lowering logic; new HIR constructs get a clear "not implemented until PR-5" panic in MIR's one exhaustive match, not new codegen work | accepted |
| D-036 | Diagnostic snapshot tests are hand-rolled (`assert_eq!` against a checked-in expected-output file per fixture), not the `insta` crate — TESTING.md's "insta-style" describes the *approach*, not a dependency requirement, and this avoids a new external dependency for a mechanism `std` already covers | accepted |
| D-037 | `pycc_testkit` and `tests/conformance/` remain deferred past PR-4 to PR-6, per D-018's original "PR-4/PR-6" window — PR-4 adds only `tests/diagnostics/` (negative fixtures), which DIAGNOSTICS.md and PYTHON_STANDARDS.md already specify independently of the conformance harness | accepted |
| D-038 | A top-level function is "public" for T0001 purposes iff its name does not start with `_`, matching ordinary Python convention — TYPE_SYSTEM.md says "locals and private helpers" are inferred but never defines "private" for module-level defs; this is the standard, least-surprising reading | accepted |
| D-039 | `pycc_types::check` registers every function's signature into the module-level `Environment` in a pass before checking any statement body, so top-level code (and, once needed, other function bodies) can call a function regardless of its definition's position in the file | accepted |
| D-040 | Function bodies resolve sibling calls against the module's shared function registry (`check_function_in`/`child_for_function`), superseding D-039's deferral -- a non-recursive call between two module-level functions now type-checks instead of raising a spurious `T0021` | accepted |
| D-041 | Function bodies also resolve reads of module-level global variables (`check()`'s 3-pass restructure: register functions, check all top-level statements, then check all function bodies against the resulting `env`), correcting a factually wrong scoping claim in D-040's own "Alternatives" section | accepted |
| D-042 | `frontend-perf-gate` CI job records a `pycc check` frontend timing baseline every run and enforces the >2% regression threshold via a hand-rolled `scripts/check_perf_regression.rb` (criterion itself does not fail its own exit code on a detected regression); it is not yet included in the required aggregate `ci-gate` | accepted |
| D-043 | PR-4's known gaps, shipped named rather than silently implied: TYPE_SYSTEM.md rule 4 (Optional/containers/narrowing) has no grammar surface yet; `help:` suggestions are never populated; every diagnostic's `Span` is still the `Span::new(0, 0)` placeholder | accepted |
| D-044 | `frontend-perf-gate` is a required `ci-gate` dependency from its first merge; this supersedes D-042's informational-only consequence because ARCHITECTURE.md and DELIVERY_PLAN.md already make a >2% regression merge-blocking | accepted |
| D-045 | Unannotated private helpers use explicit inference variables and a module-local monomorphic constraint solver; `None` is never reused as an unknown-type sentinel | accepted |
| D-046 | Pull requests restore but never publish frontend-performance caches; only a successful `push` to `main` advances the canonical baseline, preventing PR-head ratcheting | superseded by D-048 |
| D-047 | The frontend performance gate's early-start timeline is temporarily deferred to PR-6 after concurrent trust-anchor work fails to converge during PR-4 | superseded by D-048 |
| D-048 | Activate the required frontend performance gate through staged workflow trust, exact-predecessor main artifacts, and a one-head bootstrap that is removed immediately after seeding | accepted |
| D-049 | Preserve D-048's pre-split predecessor as a byte-exact inert fixture so transition tests remain independent of whichever workflow is active | accepted |
| D-050 | Pre-stage digest-selected status wording so D-048's workflow-only activation commit cannot make repository or public current-status documentation stale | accepted |
| D-051 | Replace cross-run frontend mean timing with exact predecessor/candidate medians measured on one hosted runner, staged through a byte-exact prospective workflow digest | accepted |
| D-052 | Share the module function registry across cloned type-check environments through an `Arc`-backed copy-on-write table | accepted |
| D-053 | Correct D-051's pinned artifact-ID extraction layout in a prerequisite staging merge before activation | accepted |
| D-054 | Recover the D-048 exact-base deadlock with one audited, time-bounded required-check exception and no standing bypass | accepted ("grants no reusable permission" framing narrowed by D-125) |
| D-055 | Classify function-local bindings before resolving global reads, superseding D-041's deferred `UnboundLocalError` behavior | accepted |
| D-056 | Treat paired timing as non-blocking environment telemetry only when the trusted workflow proves every executable benchmark input is identical; preserve the >2% block for every source change | accepted (identity rule retained by active D-062; D-056 workflow authorization retired) |
| D-057 | PR-5's MIR stays a typed structural mirror of HIR (not real SSA); LLVM codegen uses one `alloca` per local/parameter + `load`/`store`, relying on no optimization pass (correct and simplest for a `--debug`-only v0.1 profile per D-034/DELIVERY_PLAN.md) | accepted |
| D-058 | `int` overflow-to-bigint (D-001) is a minimal hand-rolled sign-magnitude `Vec<u32>` limb representation in `pycc_rt`, not an external bigint crate — v0.1 only needs overflow-safe arithmetic + `print`, not a general-purpose bignum API surface | accepted |
| D-059 | `str` small-string optimization (D-007) inlines up to 22 UTF-8 bytes directly in the runtime string header (no heap allocation); longer strings heap-allocate with a refcount, no interning, no rope/cow structure -- the simplest representation matching D-007's own stated `≤ 22 bytes inline` threshold | accepted |
| D-060 | `pycc_own` (ownership/escape/RC-elision) is confirmed out of scope for PR-5, per DELIVERY_PLAN.md's v0.1 crate scope; every heap-allocated `str` this PR creates is unconditionally refcounted and freed on refcount reaching zero, with no cycle collector (D-004) since no v0.1 construct can form a reference cycle without classes/containers | accepted |
| D-061 | `int`'s single `i64` storage slot (alloca/parameter/return/print) is a tagged 63-bit fixnum -- `(n << 1) \| 1` -- for the fast path; an *even* bit pattern in that same slot is a real heap `BigInt` pointer (D-058), never a raw untagged value. Every `pycc_rt_int_*` function's signature is fixed from this decision onward; only their internal fallback bodies change when Task 9 adds bigint promotion. Arithmetic/comparison codegen calls these `pycc_rt` functions rather than emitting raw LLVM int ops directly (simplest-correct for a `--debug`-only, no-perf-requirement v0.1 profile; direct-intrinsic codegen is a documented, non-blocking future optimization) | accepted |
| D-062 | Confirm changed-input paired frontend regressions with the median of five fixed complete runs per revision while retaining D-056's identical-input telemetry rule | accepted (active) |
| D-066 | Maintain an agent retrospective log and a commit-grounded session handoff log | accepted (session-handoff-log file shape narrowed by D-130) |
| D-067 | Publish the source-installed pre-commit hook from the main pycc repository rather than a separate hook repository | accepted |
| D-068 | Require a pinned local read-only review loop; asynchronous GitHub review comments remain optional | accepted |
| D-070 | Corrects D-060's own overclaim (a PR-5 Task 7 review finding, not a change of decision): "every heap-allocated `str` ... freed on refcount reaching zero" describes the intended object model, not Task 7's actual codegen scope -- refcount calls are only ever inserted at codegen-time binding sites (see D-061's Consequences for the full, corrected accepted-leak scope: unbound temporaries, loop-body-first bindings, and a never-reassigned function-scoped local all leak, memory-safely, until `pycc_own` (v0.5) lands) | accepted |
| D-071 | PR-5's own decisions are renumbered three times chasing `main`'s concurrent, unpredictable ID allocation: D-048–D-053 → D-056–D-061 (collision with `main`'s D-046–D-054) → D-057–D-064 (collision with `main`'s new D-055/D-056) → final: D-057–D-061 kept, D-062/D-066 ceded to `main`'s own unrelated new decisions of those numbers, and this branch's remaining four entries parked at D-070–D-073, a gap ahead of `main`'s current reach chosen so future `main` advances stop colliding with this branch's IDs before it merges; no decision's content, rationale, or acceptance changes, only IDs | accepted |
| D-072 | `print()`'s `None` result cannot be used as a nested expression in v0.1 (e.g. `value = print(x)`) -- `pycc_codegen` panics with a clear, named message rather than materializing a general-purpose `None` value, since no PR-5 task required one; this replaces D-035's now-closed "codegen lands in PR-5" gap as the alpha skills' tracked "intentional temporary boundary, not a reportable defect" example | accepted |
| D-073 | `pycc_codegen`'s LLVM target machine uses `RelocMode::PIC`, not `Default` -- `Default` resolves to absolute (non-PIC) addressing on Linux, which Ubuntu's PIE-by-default `cc`/`gcc` link step rejects for large-`.rodata` programs ("relocation R_X86_64_32 ... can not be used when making a PIE object"); `PIC` matches every Tier-1 platform's actual linking default uniformly | accepted |
| D-074 | MIR and LLVM codegen preserve the checked v0.1 lexical and representation contracts: completed module globals are visible inside functions, function-local targets dominate every use through entry-block allocation, and accepted `bool`→`int` boundaries materialize tagged integers | accepted |
| D-075 | Carry `None` parameters across the v0.1 user-function ABI as a canonical LLVM `i8 0` unit while retaining LLVM `void` returns | accepted |
| D-076 | Normalize every unsuccessful generated child of `pycc run` to the portable runtime-failure exit code 101 | accepted |
| D-077 | Keep project-local iEvo enable/refresh and disable symmetric through one tested helper that owns exact hook relocation and cleanup on Claude Code and Codex | accepted |
| D-078 | Make external repository monitoring checkpointed and event-driven so historical citations never become recurring poll targets | accepted |
| D-079 | `pycc check`'s `<50ms/1000 LOC` absolute-throughput floor (DELIVERY_PLAN.md row 6) is a new, separate plain-timed CLI check, not an addition to `benches/check_bench.rs`/the existing paired-regression `frontend-perf-measure`/`frontend-perf-gate` Criterion gate | accepted |
| D-080 | The conformance oracle's CI setup (PR-6, Task 6) runs after the D-014 coverage gate in `build-test-coverage`, never inside its trusted, sandboxed boundary — the gate's step prefix and script stay byte-for-byte identical to `scripts/check_roadmap_evidence.rb`'s reviewed constants | accepted |
| D-081 | Harden the project-local iEvo lifecycle against ambiguous references, unsafe filesystem redirection, and concurrent helper operations | accepted |
| D-082 | `tests/conformance.rs`'s byte-for-byte comparison strips CPython's Windows-only `\n`→`\r\n` stdio translation from the oracle's captured stdout before comparing, since that translation is a platform-specific quirk of CPython's own C runtime, not part of `print()`'s language semantics, and `pycc_rt` never performs it on any target | accepted |
| D-083 | `docs/CLI_SPEC.md`'s diagnostics-output-contract example is corrected to match `render_human`'s real, current end-to-end `pycc check` output (full-message caret label, the `Span::new(0, 0)` placeholder `line 1, column 1` span per D-043, no `help:` line) instead of an aspirational format nothing implements yet; a checked-in fixture proves the corrected example byte-for-byte (modulo the fixture's own real file path), closing DELIVERY_PLAN.md row 6's third acceptance bullet without growing new diagnostic-rendering scope | accepted |
| D-084 | PR-6, Task 7's `pycc check` absolute-throughput-floor CI step is added to `build-test-coverage` after the `cargo test --workspace` step, not immediately after `cargo build --workspace`, so it is never the first exec of the freshly-linked binary in that job | accepted |
| D-085 | PR-6's conformance checks (fib, mandelbrot-ascii vs. pinned CPython 3.14.6) live in a plain `tests/conformance.rs` integration test using `std::process::Command`, matching `tests/slice0.rs`/`tests/slice1_codegen_depth.rs`'s existing pattern — not a new `pycc_testkit` crate. `pycc_testkit` (D-018/D-037) remains deferred to whenever the full multi-version `py30`-`py315` matrix TESTING.md describes actually gets built | accepted |
| D-086 | Two deliberate type-boundary strictness decisions: (1) equality (`==`/`!=`) requires operands to be comparable under the numeric-like group (`int`/`float`/`bool` interchangeably) or `str`/`str` — the same relation ordering operators use — confirming existing behavior against `mypy --strict`'s own `comparison-overlap` check, not a new deviation; (2) numeric widening (`int` accepted where `float` is annotated) at parameters/returns/assignments requires an explicit conversion, which goes beyond even `mypy --strict` (that check accepts implicit `int`-for-`float` unconditionally, per the Python typing spec's numeric tower) — pycc has no callable `float(...)` builtin yet to perform that conversion (tracked separately, #181), so today there is no in-language way to satisfy this boundary for an `int`-typed value. Arithmetic-operator promotion (`1 + 2.5` computing `float`) is unaffected — this is about assignability of an existing value into a differently-typed slot, not operator result types — superseded in part by this entry's own 2026-08-03 corrections (`#181` shipped `float(x)`; a user-defined `float` takes priority over the builtin; a bigint-valued `int` argument aborts, matching every other numeric promotion to `float`) | accepted |
| D-087 | Relax D-022's upstream iEvo bug-report privacy scrubbing from "never contain credentials/PII/private-repo-data/raw conversation/local paths" to "never contain credentials/secrets/API keys/tokens" only — everything else may be included as-is when it makes a report more actionable, given this repository's experimental, single-maintainer status | accepted |
| D-088 | Correct three of v0.2's ROADMAP.md acceptance criteria, empirically shown unreachable or unverified before any v0.2 PR started: (1) "corpus Tier-1 compiles" replaced with a hand-authored container/generics corpus — the unmodified `tomli`/`packaging`/`more-itertools` packages need stdlib modules STDLIB_PLAN.md schedules through v0.8 (`urllib.parse`) plus several modules it does not schedule at all (`ast`, `importlib.machinery`, `sysconfig`, `types`, `operator`, `platform`, `warnings`, `bisect`, `heapq`, `collections.abc`, `email`, `keyword`, `decimal`, `fractions`, `statistics`); (2) v0.2's import support is scoped to stdlib-module intrinsic binding (`math`/`sys` only), not general resolution — general/relative multi-file imports stay v0.4 per ROADMAP.md, unchanged; (3) conformance target revised from ≥25 to ≥15 matrix rows green, based on an itemized count of PYTHON_STANDARDS.md rows v0.1+v0.2's actual surface can satisfy. Also clarifies PEP 695 in v0.2 covers generic functions and the `type` statement only — generic classes need v0.3's class support, which doesn't exist yet | accepted |
| D-089 | `pycc_hir`'s `Ty` enum (today a flat, `Copy`, six-variant scalar type consumed 729 times across `pycc_types`/`pycc_mir`/`pycc_codegen`/`pycc_hir`) becomes recursive (`Ty::List(Box<Ty>)`, `Ty::Dict(Box<Ty>, Box<Ty>)`, `Ty::Set(Box<Ty>)`, `Ty::Tuple(Vec<Ty>)`) to represent v0.2's generic container types, dropping `Copy` in favor of `Clone` at every affected call site, rather than building an arena/interning scheme this project has no evidence it needs yet | accepted |
| D-090 | PR-8, Task 5's release-mode `pycc_rt` CI-build-step digest, staged onto `main` alongside active D-084 -- found incomplete (missing the coverage-sandbox release build) before it ever activated; superseded by D-091's composed digest | accepted; superseded before activation by D-091 -- this digest was never live |
| D-091 | One composed digest, staged alongside active D-084: completes D-090's release-mode `pycc_rt` step with the missing `build-test-coverage` isolated-sandbox release build; relaxes `frontend-perf-measure`'s "Verify exact benchmark revisions" step so most of root `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` are classified (via `executable_inputs_equal`) rather than hard-aborted, while keeping `Cargo.toml`'s bench-defining `[dev-dependencies]`/`[[bench]]` tail a hard abort; and restores root-level `build.rs` classification dropped by removing the old digest function | accepted; superseded by D-100 -- D-099 (below) activated first as an independent, unrelated change and briefly retired this digest to audit-only status before D-100 composed the two |
| D-092 | `--release` also selects a release-built `pycc_rt` static library (not always `target/debug`, regardless of the flag) by threading `try_build`'s own `release` bool into `find_pycc_rt_lib_dir`/`find_pycc_rt_lib_dir_in`; CI gains a matching release-profile `pycc_rt` build step on `build-test-coverage` and every `native-build-test` leg | accepted |
| D-093 | `tests/fixtures/nbody.py`'s iteration count raised from upstream pyperformance's own `DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 20000` to `525000` to amortize the same-machine paired benchmark's fixed per-process overhead, which at 20000 mechanically compressed the measured wall-clock ratio well below the actual compute-only speedup. Even after this fix and D-092's separate `--release`/`pycc_rt` bugfix, the measured ratio plateaus at ~18.0-18.24x, short of the design spec's 20x gate — reported to the task dispatcher as BLOCKED rather than resolved by adjusting the gate or the fixture's physics | accepted |
| D-094 | PR-8 implementation forks: add `toml` crate as root-package dependency with `PyccToml` struct deserializing only v0.2-scoped fields (`[project]`/`[build]`); implement nbody harness as plain `tests/nbody_bench.rs` integration test matching conformance pattern, not a new crate or Criterion machinery; invoke `--release`'s LLVM O3 passes via `Module::run_passes("default<O3>")` with `OptimizationLevel::Aggressive` (LTO has no cross-module effect in v0.2's single-module-per-compilation model) | accepted |
| D-095 | The nbody gate stays 20x on every Tier-1 target except macOS aarch64 (`build-test-coverage`, the only Tier-1 leg on that architecture), lowered to 12x there with margin below a single observed 14.95x CI run; three local runs on real Apple Silicon hardware reproduced D-093's own ~18x plateau, ruling out fixed-overhead re-emergence -- root mechanism (leading hypothesis: `pycc_rt_float_pow`'s uninlineable call has different relative ABI overhead on aarch64 vs x86_64) is unconfirmed and tracked as a `docs/ROADMAP.md` follow-up, not resolved here | accepted |
| D-096 | `windows-latest` also gets a relaxed nbody floor (15x, not 20x): of 4 recorded CI runs, 3 measured 17.95x/17.78x/17.61x (a tight sub-20x cluster) and 1 passed at an unrecorded ratio above 20x -- read as a real ~17.6-18x ceiling with one favorable noise draw, not the reverse, but on a thinner evidentiary base than D-095's own (no independent local-hardware reproduction was possible); leading hypothesis (Windows Defender re-scanning/hashing the freshly built unsigned binary on every launch, frequency unconfirmed) is unconfirmed and tracked alongside D-095's own `docs/ROADMAP.md` follow-up, not resolved here | accepted |
| D-097 | `tests/nbody_bench.rs`'s speedup gate previously only checked that both programs exited successfully, never that they computed the same values -- a miscompile that runs fast but wrong could have passed. Adds one untimed correctness check (build once, run pycc and the CPython oracle once each via `Command::output()`, byte-compare after stripping CPython's Windows `\r\n` translation exactly like `tests/conformance.rs` already does) before the existing `RUNS = 5` timed loops, which stay untouched so D-095/D-096's measured gate floors remain valid | superseded by D-098 |
| D-098 | D-097's byte-exact comparison failed the very next CI run on `windows-latest`: pycc and CPython disagreed with each other and with D-093's macOS-recorded value, all agreeing to ~10 significant digits before diverging -- consistent with cross-platform `pow`/libm rounding differences amplified by this fixture's own chaotic dynamics, not a miscompile. Replaces the comparison with `1e-6`-relative-tolerance equality on the 3 parsed floats (worst observed relative gap ~7.4e-10, ~3 orders of margin below the tolerance), keeping D-097's untimed-check placement and CRLF-stripping unchanged | accepted |
| D-099 | Add a main-seeded, pull-request-restore-only vcpkg binary cache around D-027's Windows libxml2 build, with an exact hosted-image/toolchain/vcpkg identity and a two-PR stage-then-activate boundary | accepted; active until D-100 composed it with D-091 |
| D-100 | Compose D-091's release-mode `pycc_rt`/relaxed-manifest changes with D-099's Windows vcpkg cache into one new reviewed digest, since D-099 activated first (an independent, unrelated change) and left D-091 without a live activation path on its own | accepted; activated (via stage PR #231 + this PR-8 merge) |
| D-101 | Lower the nbody gate to 18x on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, from 6 CI observations (4 failures tightly clustered at 19.86x-19.92x, 2 passes at unrecorded ratios); mechanism unconfirmed, unlike D-095/D-096 | accepted |
| D-102 | Extend `tests/conformance.rs` for PR-9's 9 new PEP fixtures (no `pycc_testkit` crate); `PYTHON_STANDARDS.md` status-column flips require observed all-target, both-profile CI evidence, recorded by hand pending real automation | accepted |
| D-103 | Keep the search-policy trust anchor and its complete protected input bundle base-owned; every successor change uses a non-shrinking two-merge manifest protocol and every candidate rejects checkout-affecting `.gitattributes` | accepted |
| D-104 | Defer D-089's unresolved generic function type-parameter `Ty` placeholder to PR-13; PR-10's own recursive `Ty` variants are exactly `List`/`Dict`/`Set`/`Tuple`, nothing else | accepted |
| D-105 | v0.2's `list[T]` thin slice (PR-10): no annotation syntax, read-only indexing, `.append()`/`len()` as hand-recognized special forms (not general method dispatch), `ForList` desugars to an index-counted loop reusing `ForRange`'s codegen, and codegen ships `list[int]` only (`T0034` pre-codegen diagnostic for every other element type) | accepted |
| D-106 | `PyIntListObj` (PR-10 Task 10) stores raw, untagged `i64` elements, not D-061's tagged representation — an exception analogous to `bool`'s own untagged `i8`. `pycc_codegen` (Task 11) owns the boundary conversion; a bigint-tagged `int` reaching `.append()` or `[i]` is an honest runtime panic, not silent corruption | accepted |
| D-107 | `list[T]` pointers get their own `pycc_codegen::Scalar::List` variant (Task 11a), replacing a `Scalar::Str` reuse that was safe only until `if x:`/`print(x)` on a list became reachable (confirmed they are); `truthy`/`to_str`/list-arithmetic get honest panics. List refcounting stays leak-only in v0.2 — no `pycc_rt_int_list_incref`/`_decref` call site exists yet — tracked as a `ROADMAP.md` follow-up, not silently assumed solved | accepted |
| D-108 | `pycc_rt_int_list_get` rejects any negative index as out-of-range (Task 10), matching the same panic as a too-large positive index; real Python's `lst[-1]`-as-last-element is out of scope for v0.2. Recorded as its own decision (Task 12) after Task 10's review found the behavior misattributed to "D-105," which never actually discusses index sign or bounds | accepted |
| D-109 | PR-10's own `frontend-perf-gate` failed on two independent, fully-fresh CI measurements (4.7008%, 5.1960%; threshold 2.00%) on a scalar-only fixture with zero `list[T]` involvement, initially read as a confirmed real, self-inflicted regression from D-089/D-104's recursive `Ty` migration. Boxing `Ty::Dict`/`Ty::Tuple` (Task 14) shrank `size_of::<Ty>()` from 24 to 16 bytes, a real representation improvement — but the CI evidence that this closed the gate did not hold up under a full log review (a non-independent cached duplicate measurement, plus 4 further failing measurements on commits untouched by `Ty`'s representation, one pre-fix pair showing a zero-code-change pass/fail flip); see this entry's own "Correction" note. PR #236 is merge-blocked again pending a methodology decision, tracked as [#109](https://github.com/rotnov/pycc/issues/109) | accepted |
| D-110 | A call target resolves through the active value binding before builtin/function-registry fallback (#133), callee-first in diagnostic order; with D-041's final-module-environment body checking this deliberately rejects some later-rebind programs CPython's dynamic order would run (numbered after open PR #236's published D-104–D-109 claims) | accepted |
| D-111 | Issue priority is the issue title's leading `P1:`/`P2:`/`P3:` prefix, not a GitHub label — this repository has never had priority labels; `issue-select`'s scoring order states the live mechanism directly instead of checking (and finding empty) GitHub labels first | accepted |
| D-112 | Move `frontend-perf-measure`/`frontend-perf-gate` from `macos-14` to `ubuntu-latest`, backed by real shadow-measurement evidence gathered before activation, resolving issue #109's hosted-runner false-positive regressions | accepted |
| D-113 | Reject ancestor symlinks and mount points in the raw `--root` CLI argument, strengthening D-081 after issue #169 was reopened by a post-merge review finding the original leaf-only fix incomplete | accepted |
| D-114 | Raise `frontend-perf-gate`'s regression threshold from 2.0% to 7.0% to accommodate PR-10's real, accepted `Ty`-migration cost (D-109); redoes a first attempt (PR #286/#290, reverted via #291) that deadlocked by staging three D-103 manifest targets in one round instead of per-target propose/activate pairs | accepted |
| D-115 | tuple[...] v0.2 representation is a fixed-arity LLVM struct-of-scalar-fields, held as an SSA aggregate value (not a heap pointer) — no new heap object, no refcounting question, since D-116 excludes `str` fields | accepted |
| D-116 | tuple[...] v0.2 scope: `int`/`bool`/`float` elements only, any arity/mix (`T0039`); `t[k]` requires a literal non-negative in-range integer index (`T0040`); iteration, unpacking, and annotation syntax deferred to ROADMAP; function-argument/return pass-through is codegen-ready but, per this entry's own "Correction" note, not reachable from real source yet — `pycc_types`' private-helper solver is scalar-only, a pre-existing gap shared by `list`/`dict`/`set` | accepted |
| D-117 | List/dict/set comprehensions are statement-level (`Stmt::Assign`-RHS-only), reusing `ForList`'s existing iterable polymorphism; the loop variable is rewritten to a synthesized, collision-proof internal name so it cannot leak into an enclosing same-named binding (PEP 709's own observable guarantee) | accepted |
| D-118 | `list[int]` slicing (`xs[a:b:c]`) ships with non-negative runtime bounds, CPython-matching clamping, and a positive step; negative bounds/non-positive step are honest runtime panics, extending D-108's precedent; `dict`/`set`/`tuple` slicing stay unsupported (the first two correctly, per real Python's own `TypeError`; `tuple` as a genuine deferral) | accepted |
| D-119 | `list.pop()`, `dict.get(key, default)`, `set.add(value)` ship as v0.2's "remaining container methods depth"; `tuple` gets none; `in`-based set membership stays deferred (re-affirms D-123); every new rejection path reuses an existing `T0021`/`T0033`/`T0034`/`T0036`/`T0038` diagnostic — zero new `T0xxx` codes | accepted |
| D-120 | The PEP-709 conformance fixture demonstrates the one CPython-observable, statically-testable guarantee PEP 709 depends on (a comprehension's own loop variable does not leak into an enclosing same-named binding) rather than literal bytecode-inlining, which has no analog in pycc's architecture; container `to_str`/`truthy` stay explicitly out of this PR's scope, so the fixture prints element-wise | accepted |
| D-121 | dict[K, V]/set[T] v0.2 representation is a dense insertion-ordered array with linear-scan lookup, not a hash table — the real hash-table design (`docs/RUNTIME.md`'s "swiss table" wording) remains the v1.0 target | accepted |
| D-122 | dict[K, V]/set[T] key/element types are scoped to existing equality-comparable scalars; exactly one combination (`dict[str, int]`, `set[int]`) gets real codegen, everything else type-checks structurally but is rejected pre-codegen (`T0036`/`T0038`) | accepted |
| D-123 | dict[str, int] ships a `d[k] = v` insert-or-update operation (proving insertion order survives mutation, not just literal order); set[int] ships no membership test and no CPython iteration-order claim (blocked on the `in` operator, tracked as a ROADMAP follow-up) | accepted |
| D-124 | dict[str, int]/set[int] refcounting stays leak-only, extending D-107's exact reasoning to two new heap object types (`PyDictObj`/`PyIntSetObj`) | accepted |
| D-125 | Session-driven temporary CI-check relaxation via `scripts/manage_ci_bypass.py`, narrowly superseding D-024/D-054 for exactly one publicly tracked, expiry-bound incident at a time | accepted |
| D-126 | Gather exact-child CPU-time evidence (Phase A) alongside the existing wall-clock nbody gate before changing its assertion or per-target floors (Phase B) | accepted (Phase A) |
| D-127 | Agents own the full delivery lifecycle for a task without pausing to ask the repository owner; a genuine judgment fork is resolved via an independent reviewer instead, not by soliciting the owner — carving out D-024's emergency path, credential/account actions, and any other owner-reserved action unchanged | accepted |
| D-128 | Preserve ordinary Python imports while automatically classifying and bundling permitted CPython-backed dependencies under closed `auto`/`allowlist`/`deny` policies | accepted (planned v0.7; not implemented) |
| D-129 | Keep the nbody assertion on wall clock and retain the existing per-target floors after five observations per Tier-1 leg show CPU time leaves Ubuntu x86_64 variance unchanged and worsens Windows variance | accepted |
| D-130 | Decompose the single, ever-growing `docs/SESSION_LOG.md` into one dated file per checkpoint under `docs/sessions/`, discovered by directory listing rather than a maintained index | accepted |
| D-131 | Store already-materializable `None` values in canonical `i8 0` function-local and module-global assignment slots while preserving separate initialization guards and D-072's nested-`print()` rejection | accepted |
| D-132 | Measure the D-084 `pycc check` throughput floor as the median of 3 wall-clock samples instead of a single sample, after historical CI evidence showed the single-sample design was sound but exposed to rare single-draw runner noise | accepted |
| D-133 | PR-13 generic-function type-parameter representation: a new `Ty::Param(String)` variant, resolved to a concrete `Ty` per call site by substitution before `pycc_mir` lowering — not a new `Ty::Infer`-like unification variable | proposed |
| D-134 | PR-13 thin slice: exactly one generic function, callable at any number of call sites, each independently monomorphized, restricted to a single type parameter bound to one of `Ty::Int`/`Ty::Float`/`Ty::Bool`/`Ty::Str` per call site; every other shape (multiple type parameters, a type parameter appearing in a container position, an unresolvable call site) is rejected pre-codegen with a new `T00xx` diagnostic | proposed |
| D-135 | The `type` statement and legacy `TypeAlias` are a compile-time-only name substitution inside `pycc_types`; neither produces a `pycc_hir`/`pycc_mir` node, a runtime representation, nor a new `Ty` variant | proposed |

## Template

```
## D-0XX: Title
- Status: proposed
- Context: what forces the choice
- Decision: what we do
- Alternatives: what we rejected and why
- Consequences: what gets easier / harder / irreversible
```

Entries D-001…D-013 get their long-form sections as they graduate to `accepted` (first PR that depends on the decision must include it).

## D-014: 100% test coverage requirement

- Status: accepted (PR-1/PR-2 wires the CI gate this depends on)
- Context: pycc is a compiler — silent gaps in its own test suite are exactly the kind of bug that surfaces as a miscompile in someone else's code, far from where the gap was introduced. The project needs a binary, CI-enforced floor, not an aspirational target. Verified on the pinned toolchain (rustc 1.97.1) before adopting: `#[coverage(off)]` is still gated behind `#![feature(coverage_attribute)]` (rust-lang/rust#84605) and unavailable on stable — so any exemption mechanism must not depend on it.
- Decision: `cargo llvm-cov` (wraps `-C instrument-coverage`; a separately distributed cargo subcommand that *drives* the `llvm-tools-preview` rustup component at runtime — the two are installed separately, caught by repo audit issue #13 after an earlier draft of this entry conflated them — independent of the Homebrew LLVM 22.1.1 used for `inkwell` codegen, no version coupling between the two) gates every PR at `--fail-under-lines 100 --fail-under-regions 100`. Branch coverage is reported when available but not gated — it requires a nightly toolchain, and this project has no other reason to leave the stable channel (`rust-toolchain.toml` pins stable 1.97.1; introducing a nightly dependency just for one coverage sub-metric would be its own separate, unwarranted decision). The only exemption granularity is whole-file, via `--ignore-filename-regex`, because per-function exemption has no stable mechanism — this is a feature, not a limitation: it keeps platform-conditional or otherwise-unreachable code in its own file rather than letting an exemption hide inside an otherwise-normal one. Test code itself (`tests/`, `*_tests.rs`, `tests.rs`) is excluded from the denominator by cargo-llvm-cov by default, so coverage measures product code exercised by tests, not tests covering themselves.
- Alternatives: `grcov`/`tarpaulin` (older, less precise than LLVM source-based coverage now that `cargo llvm-cov` exists and the project already standardizes on LLVM tooling); a percentage target below 100% (rejected — a threshold like 95% lets the uncovered 5% drift to wherever it's least inconvenient to test, which is usually exactly the error-handling and edge-case code that matters most for a compiler); no gate at all (rejected outright, contradicts D-013's premise that specs and quality bars must be mechanically checkable).
- Consequences: every new file needs tests before merge, including CI/tooling glue where that's checked into a coverable crate. Genuinely untestable code (OS-specific branches exercised only on a different Tier-1 target, for instance) must live in its own file and get a named, justified entry in the exemption list in TESTING.md — an undocumented exemption is a review-blocking finding, same as an undocumented CPython deviation elsewhere in the spec. Wired into CI starting PR-1 (see DELIVERY_PLAN.md), not retrofitted later, so no crate ever accumulates an uncovered backlog.

## D-015: LLVM/inkwell version pin

- Status: accepted (PR-1/PR-2 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: ARCHITECTURE.md/D-002 specify "LLVM (inkwell)" without a version. `pycc_codegen` needs one concrete pin to build against at all. Checked empirically on the dev host rather than picked from memory: only one real LLVM install exists (`llvm@17` through `llvm@22` under `/opt/homebrew/opt/` all resolve to the same single Cellar keg, `llvm/22.1.1` — not five distinct installs).
- Decision: LLVM `22.1.1`, `inkwell = "0.9"` (latest at pin time) with `default-features = false, features = ["llvm22-1"]`. `inkwell` 0.9.0's feature list was checked directly (crates.io version metadata) to confirm `llvm22-1` exists before committing to it — it does, alongside `llvm19-1`/`llvm20-1`/`llvm21-1`.
- Alternatives: none genuinely competing — this isn't a preference call between installed options, only one LLVM was actually present. The alternative considered was installing an older/different LLVM version to chase a "more battle-tested" inkwell feature; rejected as unnecessary extra setup work for no concrete benefit given `llvm22-1` already exists and matches what's on the machine.
- Consequences: `pycc_codegen/Cargo.toml`'s `inkwell` feature and CI's `LLVM_SYS_221_PREFIX` (or whatever exact env var `llvm-sys` reports needing — confirm per-build, don't assume the number matches the LLVM minor version) must be changed together if this ever bumps; a version bump is a new ADR, not an edit to this one.

## D-016: Vendored parser version pin

- Status: accepted (PR-1/PR-2 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: D-003 already decided *that* pycc vendors `ruff_python_parser` through v0.5; it didn't pin *which* version. `ruff_python_parser`/`ruff_python_ast` are published to crates.io infrequently (6 total versions as of this pin) rather than continuously, so drift between what a contributor's local `Cargo.lock` resolves and what CI resolves is a real risk without an explicit pin.
- Decision: `ruff_python_parser = "0.0.6"`, `ruff_python_ast = "0.0.6"` — the newest versions on crates.io when PR-1 was built, verified via the crates.io API rather than assumed from training data.
- Alternatives: pinning to a specific git commit of `astral-sh/ruff` directly (rejected for v0.1 — crates.io releases are simpler to audit and update than a floating git dependency, and the project isn't blocked on any unreleased fix yet).
- Consequences: `pycc_ast`/`pycc_parser`'s `Cargo.toml` pins these exactly; bumping either is a new ADR entry (or an amendment noted here), not a silent `cargo update`.

## D-017: No separate `pycc_lexer` crate for v0.1

- Status: accepted (PR-1/PR-2 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: ARCHITECTURE.md's crate table lists `pycc_lexer`/`pycc_parser`/`pycc_ast` as three crates, describing the end state after D-003 resolves (pycc's own hand-written lexer and parser, v0.6). During the vendored-parser bootstrap phase, `ruff_python_parser` performs lexing internally and does not expose a standalone token stream pycc could wrap even if it wanted to, and nothing downstream in the v0.1 pipeline (`pycc_hir` onward) consumes tokens directly — only the parsed AST.
- Decision: v0.1 creates `pycc_ast` (a thin, stable re-export boundary over `ruff_python_ast`) and `pycc_parser` (wraps `ruff_python_parser::parse_module`), but not `pycc_lexer`.
- Alternatives: an empty pass-through `pycc_lexer` crate now, for table-completeness with ARCHITECTURE.md (rejected as YAGNI — nothing would depend on it, and an empty crate that exists only to match a table is exactly the kind of unnecessary abstraction the project's own engineering conventions warn against).
- Consequences: `pycc_lexer` gets created in v0.6 when D-003's own-parser work gives it a real reason to exist (a token stream something actually consumes); until then, ARCHITECTURE.md's crate table describes a target state, not every crate's exact creation date.

## D-018: `pycc_testkit` deferred past PR-1/PR-2

- Status: proposed (graduates to accepted when `pycc_testkit` is actually created)
- Context: DELIVERY_PLAN.md's original "v0.1 crate scope" and "Testing scope for v0.1" sections listed `pycc_testkit` as built alongside the other 8 crates, with Layer 2 (the conformance harness) starting "as early as slice 0" -- but PR-1/PR-2 never created it (flagged by repo audit as an undocumented deviation, unlike `pycc_lexer`'s reasoned D-017). TESTING.md's actual spec for `pycc_testkit` is a real harness: `.py` fixtures under `tests/conformance/pyXY/` with PEP/category/milestone header comments, diffed against a pinned CPython 3.14 reference output, flipping ✅ marks in PYTHON_STANDARDS.md. None of that exists yet -- PYTHON_STANDARDS.md's matrix is still 100% unstarted (☐), so there is no PEP-level conformance tracking for a harness to drive yet.
- Decision: PR-1/PR-2 use a hand-written `tests/slice0.rs` integration test instead -- it compiles fixtures through the real `pycc` binary and asserts exact stdout, which is a genuine (if narrow) proof that the pipeline works end to end, but it is not TESTING.md's Layer 2 harness. `pycc_testkit` gets built for real once PR-4/PR-6 gives it PEPs to actually track (PR-6 is where DELIVERY_PLAN.md already schedules the first real conformance run: fib + mandelbrot-ascii vs. CPython on all 5 targets).
- Alternatives: scaffold a minimal `pycc_testkit` crate now, even with nothing real for it to do (rejected as premature -- there's no PEP matrix yet for it to check against, so it would be structure without function, the same YAGNI concern D-017 raised about `pycc_lexer`); keep DELIVERY_PLAN.md's "as early as slice 0" wording as-is and treat `tests/slice0.rs` as satisfying it (rejected -- that wording specifically promises TESTING.md's Layer 2 shape, which `tests/slice0.rs` doesn't have, and leaving the mismatch undocumented is exactly the kind of thing this decisions log exists to prevent).
- Consequences: DELIVERY_PLAN.md's "Testing scope for v0.1" section is corrected to say Layer 2 starts at PR-4/PR-6, not slice 0. `tests/slice0.rs` stays as ad hoc coverage for the two named PR-2 fixtures specifically, not a stand-in for the conformance harness.

## D-019: Codex and Claude Code are equal agent surfaces

- Status: accepted
- Context: pycc development uses both OpenAI Codex and Claude Code. Letting repository-owned automation exist on only one surface would make the effective development contract depend on which client happened to open a task, while copying complete instruction files per client would let them drift.
- Decision: `AGENTS.md` is the canonical shared repository instruction source and `CLAUDE.md` imports it. Every new or changed repository-owned agent or skill ships discoverable entrypoints and equivalent behavior, safety gates, inputs, and outputs for both Codex and Claude Code in the same pull request. Platform-specific adapters may differ, but they remain thin and share the underlying implementation where practical.
- Alternatives: choose one agent platform (rejected because both are active development surfaces); duplicate all rules and implementations independently (rejected because parity would be unverifiable and drift-prone); permit silent single-platform gaps (rejected because task behavior would become client-dependent).
- Consequences: agent/skill changes are incomplete until discovery plus primary success and failure paths work on both surfaces. A platform API gap needs a safe documented fallback, not an implicit reduction in support.

## D-020: Pull-request CI is an untrusted execution boundary

- Status: accepted
- Context: workflows may execute scripts and definitions controlled by a pull request, including same-repository branches. Workflow-level write permissions, OIDC, secrets, environments, or inherited state can therefore expose deployment and repository authority before review. A checker executed only from the pull-request revision cannot be its own trust anchor because that revision can change the checker too.
- Decision: every workflow declares an explicit workflow-level read/none permission baseline. Jobs that execute or consume untrusted pull-request state receive no elevated capability beyond a minimally scoped read-only `GITHUB_TOKEN`. Every privileged job, including one in a push-only or reusable workflow, is isolated behind the exact `push` plus `refs/heads/main` guard. The semantic policy checker provides normal CI feedback, while a separate read-only `pull_request_target` workflow runs the checker from the trusted base commit and downloads only head-revision workflow YAML as non-executable data. The checker also requires the trust-anchor workflow itself to match an independently reviewed SHA-256 allowlist.
- Alternatives: rely on repository permission defaults (rejected because they are mutable and implicit); trust an in-branch grep checker (rejected because YAML spellings bypass text matching and the PR can modify its own checker); grant workflow-level credentials and skip only the deploy step (rejected because credentials exist before step conditions contain their use).
- Consequences: after the one-time manually reviewed bootstrap PR introduces the trust anchor, workflow changes must pass the trusted `Workflow policy` check from their base revision. Cross-job artifacts, caches, outputs, reusable workflows, secrets, and environments remain explicit review boundaries even when the static checker accepts the YAML.

## D-021: Agent task preflight and documentation refresh

- Status: accepted
- Context: this AI-first repository treats specifications and generated Rust API documentation as implementation inputs. Starting from a stale remote ref, stale rustdoc, or an unidentified dirty tree can make an otherwise-correct agent optimize against the wrong contract or overwrite user work.
- Decision: before a new task mutates the repository, the agent records status and HEAD, fetches/prunes without changing checked-out files, resolves the remote default branch, starts from that exact commit in a clean task branch/worktree, generates `cargo doc --workspace --no-deps`, and reads the specification/rustdoc that owns the affected area. Continuing dirty work is allowed only in place and only after comparing it with refreshed remote state; no implicit pull, merge, rebase, reset, or branch switch is authorized.
- Authority and scope: the preflight authorizes read-only Git/network discovery, creation of an isolated task branch/worktree, and local generated rustdoc. It does not authorize integrating upstream changes into a dirty tree, publishing a branch, or changing external systems.
- Privacy and failure behavior: preflight output stays in the task transcript and must not publish local paths or repository content. A failed fetch or documentation build is recorded and understood; older generated documentation must not be represented as current.
- Rollback: the isolated worktree/branch can be removed after preserving any intended commits. Changing this mandatory sequence requires a superseding decision because every later agent task depends on it.

## D-022: Autonomous public iEvo bug reporting

- Status: accepted (privacy and failure behavior clause below superseded by [D-087](#d-087-relax-ievo-bug-report-privacy-scrubbing-to-credentials-only) — see that decision for the current scrubbing rule; every other clause here remains in force unchanged)
- Context: reproducible defects in the iEvo control plane can affect every agent task until upstream fixes ship. Requiring a new permission round-trip after the user has adopted the project policy delays correction and encourages local-only workarounds that other users cannot discover.
- Decision: after reproducing an iEvo malfunction, contradiction, broken hook, or invalid command and searching open and closed upstream issues, agents may create or update a public `ievo-ai/skills` issue without additional per-report permission. Duplicates, expected behavior, unverified suspicion, and ordinary pycc failures are not reportable.
- Authority and scope: the standing authority covers only the minimum useful iEvo report and evidence. It does not authorize publishing unrelated pycc material, reporting to other projects, or changing upstream code/labels/issue state beyond the report itself.
- Privacy and failure behavior (superseded — see D-087): this clause originally required reports to omit credentials, personal data, private/proprietary content, raw conversations, and identifying local paths even when the pycc repository is public. D-087 narrows this to credentials/secrets/API keys/tokens only; everything else may be included as-is when it makes a report more actionable. The failure-handling half of this clause is unchanged by D-087: if authentication/network submission fails, preserve the pending sanitized report locally and surface the failure; do not repeatedly spam the endpoint.
- Rollback: a public report cannot be made private retroactively. A mistaken report is corrected and closed with an explicit explanation. Revoking standing authority requires a superseding decision and removal of the matching AGENTS.md section.

## D-023: Shared auto-evolution intent with local hook execution

- Status: accepted
- Context: the repository wants correction-driven evolution to survive across contributors, but iEvo generates hook scripts under gitignored `.ievo/hooks/`. Committing shared hook entries that invoke those absent targets made every clean clone emit hook errors (issue #28; upstream `ievo-ai/skills#446`).
- Decision: `.ievo/evo-auto.flag` records shared intent with `signal: corrections-only` and `auto_write_scope: project-wide-only`. Generated scripts and their hook wiring are machine-local: `.ievo/hooks/` and `.claude/settings.local.json` are ignored, while shared `.claude/settings.json` may reference only tracked targets or tracked fail-silent wrappers. Inline interpreter forms such as `sh -c`, `python -c`, and `node --eval` are forbidden in shared hooks because their executable targets cannot be validated statically. Agents validate the tracked-file view in CI.
- Authority and scope: automatic capture may record confirmed user corrections under the configured project-wide scope. It does not broaden authority for external writes, source-code changes, secrets collection, or arbitrary per-agent behavior changes. Candidate lessons outside the auto-write scope remain reviewable candidates.
- Privacy and failure behavior: local hook inputs and generated logs remain local/ignored. Missing local setup must be a silent no-op in shared state, never a failing committed command. Validation failure blocks merge.
- Rollback: run the iEvo auto-disable workflow locally, remove or supersede the shared flag, remove local hook wiring/scripts, and verify the clean tracked view. A future tracked-wrapper design requires a superseding decision and security review.

## D-024: Protected main and audited emergency bypass

- Status: accepted (the "not delegated to routine tasks" clause in "Authority and scope" below is narrowly superseded by [D-125](#d-125-session-driven-temporary-ci-check-relaxation-narrowly-superseding-d-024d-054) for exactly that decision's own session-driven temporary-bypass mechanism — see that decision for the current scope; every other clause here remains in force unchanged)
- Context: commit `0ac9b1d` reached `main` while PR #3 remained open after a timed-out merge request, proving that commit messages and monitoring conventions do not create a review boundary. An AI-authored compiler needs the repository host—not agent intent—to enforce the PR/CI/review path. The repository currently has one maintainer, and GitHub does not count an author's approval of their own pull request.
- Decision: protect `main` for administrators and automation alike. Require an up-to-date pull request, the current `build-test-coverage` status, and resolved conversations, and disallow force pushes/deletion. Set required approving reviews to zero while there is only one maintainer; enable one independent approval, stale-review dismissal, and last-push approval when a second human maintainer is available. A push-triggered audit runs the pre-push checker (or an immutable reviewed bootstrap when that parent predates the checker) and requires every introduced commit to correlate with a merged-main PR whose merge commit arrived in the same push; a historical commit-to-PR association alone is insufficient. Because GitHub loads a push workflow from the pushed revision, the external repository monitor independently verifies the workflow content and expected run. New required checks are added to protection only after they have run successfully on `main`.
- Authority and scope: normal agent/bot credentials may open/update PRs but cannot bypass protection. Repository administrators retain the platform ability to edit protection only for the documented emergency procedure; that authority is not delegated to routine tasks.
- Privacy and failure behavior: the audit publishes only repository-native commit/PR identifiers. A direct or unassociated commit, changed audit workflow, missing expected run, or failed run requires a governance incident and blocks releases until history/protection are reconciled. CI/provider or external-monitor outages delay release rather than weakening requirements.
- Rollback: emergency relaxation is time-bounded, linked to an incident, records before/after settings, and restores protection immediately after recovery. Permanently weakening the rule requires a superseding decision and explicit review.

## D-025: Registered contracts for shared hook targets

- Status: accepted
- Context: D-023 permits a tracked fail-silent wrapper, but merely finding a hook target in Git does not prove that it tolerates an absent machine-local dependency. A tracked script or symlink can still invoke a missing `.ievo/hooks/` file and fail in every clean clone.
- Decision: this entry supersedes D-023 only for admitting shared hook targets. Every tracked filesystem target referenced by shared hook configuration must appear in `FAIL_SILENT_WRAPPER_CONTRACTS`, paired with a tracked `scripts/test_*.py` contract that is discovered by the required agent-policy test run. The contract must exercise the target from a clean-clone simulation with local generated hooks absent and assert a silent successful no-op. The registry is empty until such a wrapper and its security review are delivered.
- Authority and scope: registration permits only the named target in shared hook configuration. It does not permit generated hooks, local settings, arbitrary inline code, shell control operators, absolute or home-relative paths, loader URLs, or untracked files.
- Privacy and failure behavior: wrapper contracts use synthetic paths and inputs and must not access user state, credentials, or the network. A missing target, missing contract test, undiscovered test path, or unregistered target fails CI.
- Rollback: remove the shared hook entry and registry record together. Weakening the registry or accepting a wrapper without its clean-clone contract requires a superseding decision and security review.

## D-026: PR-3's cross-compilation proof is same-OS cross-arch, not cross-OS

- Status: accepted (PR-3 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: D-011/README.md/ARCHITECTURE.md/CLI_SPEC.md require `pycc build --target <triple>` cross-compilation from v0.1, "bundled lld, no system linker needed." Checked empirically before committing to a design: `cc -target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` on this macOS host fails immediately (`stdio.h` file not found) -- cross-compiling to a *different OS* needs that OS's actual sysroot (headers, libc, CRT startup objects) physically present on the build host, which "bundle a linker" does not provide by itself; tools that do this for real (`cross-rs`, Zig) bundle or download full target sysroots, a substantially bigger undertaking than wiring up an existing capability. By contrast, `cc -target x86_64-apple-macos12` on this same arm64 host succeeded immediately and produced a genuinely running x86_64 Mach-O binary (verified via Rosetta 2) -- macOS's own SDK already covers both Tier-1 Mac architectures, and Rust's own target support cross-builds `pycc_rt` for `x86_64-apple-darwin` from an `aarch64-apple-darwin` host cleanly via plain `rustup target add` + `cargo build --target`.
- Decision: PR-3's "at least one cross-compiled build" is same-OS, cross-architecture: build for `x86_64-apple-darwin` from the `macos-14` (arm64) CI runner (or the reverse), execute the result on the matching native runner (`macos-15-intel`). This is genuine cross-compilation (a different `--target` than the host, a real architecture-mismatched binary, executed and verified on its own native hardware) using exactly the mechanism the spec describes (`pycc build --target <triple>`), just scoped to the pair of targets where "no system linker needed" is actually true today. `pycc_codegen::compile_to_object` itself is not scoped to any OS pair -- it accepts any target triple LLVM knows about (`Target::initialize_all` covers whatever the LLVM build supports) -- so extending to cross-OS later is a linking/sysroot problem, not a codegen rewrite.
- Alternatives: attempt full cross-OS compilation now with a hand-rolled minimal sysroot (rejected -- fragile, would need maintaining libc/CRT stubs per target, and is exactly the kind of "half-working hack" this project's own conventions warn against); skip the cross-compilation requirement entirely for PR-3 and defer all of it (rejected -- issue #9 exists specifically because a previous draft of this plan silently dropped this requirement; the same-arch proof is a real, honest partial fix, not another silent drop).
- Consequences: Windows MSVC and Linux cross-compilation (from a macOS or from each other) remain a real gap for a specific future PR, once sysroot bundling/downloading is designed properly -- tracked as a known gap here, not silently reintroduced. The CI matrix's other 3 targets (Linux x64/arm64, Windows) still build+test natively on their own runners in PR-3; only the *cross*-compiled proof is scoped to the Mac pair.

## D-027: Windows build needs a real `libxml2`, supplied via vcpkg

- Status: accepted (PR-3 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: `native-build-test (windows-latest)` failed `cargo build --workspace` with `LINK : fatal error LNK1181: cannot open input file 'xml2s.lib'`, after the LLVM install and path-handling itself succeeded. Traced to `llvm-sys-221.0.1/build.rs`'s `get_system_libraries` (around line 298), which runs the downloaded release's own `llvm-config --system-libs` and emits a `cargo:rustc-link-lib=...` for every name it returns -- `xml2s` is one of them, because LLVM's official prebuilt Windows 22.1.1 archive was itself built with libxml2 support. This is a property of the official binary, not something `llvm-sys` or this project chose; there is no Cargo feature to opt the dependency away, and rebuilding LLVM from source without it is a categorically bigger undertaking than the whole rest of PR-3.
- Decision: install a real `libxml2` on the Windows runner via vcpkg (preinstalled on GitHub-hosted runners, path given by the `VCPKG_INSTALLATION_ROOT` env var -- not hardcoded, since it differs across runner-image versions), using the `x64-windows-static-md` triplet: a static `.lib` (not a DLL) built against the *dynamic* MSVC CRT, matching Rust's own default linkage for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (which links the release, dynamic CRT regardless of `cargo build` vs `cargo build --release`). The resulting `libxml2.lib` is copied to `xml2s.lib` in the same directory (whatever exact name the port produces -- matched via a glob rather than hardcoded, since vcpkg's own file naming wasn't going to be verified against a live Windows host before pushing) and that directory is appended to the `LIB` environment variable, which `link.exe` reads natively for extra search paths -- no rustflags or `.cargo/config.toml` needed.
- Alternatives: ship an empty stub `xml2s.lib` (just enough to satisfy the linker's ability to *open* the file) -- rejected as too likely to just trade this error for a less obvious "unresolved external symbol xmlXXXX" one further down the same link, if any actually-linked LLVM object genuinely calls into libxml2, costing another full CI round-trip to re-diagnose; a real, fully-functional library resolves either way. Building LLVM from source with `LLVM_ENABLE_LIBXML2=OFF` -- rejected, hours-scale effort for a one-line dependency. Dropping Windows from the CI matrix -- rejected outright: D-011 makes Windows/MSVC Tier-1 from v0.1, non-negotiable.
- Consequences: adds one more external package install to the Windows leg of `native-build-test`, and one more platform-specific quirk to maintain alongside the Linux Polly and Windows path-mangling fixes already in `ci.yml`. If a future LLVM/inkwell version bump ships a Windows release built *without* libxml2, this step becomes a harmless no-op dependency rather than a hard requirement -- worth rechecking whenever D-015 is next touched.

## D-028: Windows has no system `cc`, and clang's bare-invocation default target is unreliable

- Status: accepted (PR-3 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: with D-027 resolved, `cargo build --workspace` passed on Windows but `cargo test --workspace` failed most of `tests/slice0.rs`'s integration tests -- every one that runs `pycc build`/`pycc run` end-to-end -- and, discovered a CI round later after the first fix below landed, four unit tests inside `pycc_codegen`'s own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` kept failing with the identical symptom. Several distinct, real bugs surfaced across multiple CI round-trips, not test flakiness, each verified directly rather than guessed at:
  1. `try_build` (`src/main.rs`) hardcoded `Command::new("cc")` to invoke the second-stage linker (object file + `pycc_rt` → native binary). Windows has no `cc` by default -- that is a Unix convention; MSVC's own tools are `cl.exe`/`link.exe`. Confirmed this was the *only* new problem at this step by checking that `native-build-test` already passes on both Linux architectures and macOS, i.e. `cc` genuinely already works everywhere else.
  2. `find_pycc_rt_lib_dir_in` hardcoded the expected static-lib filename as `libpycc_rt.a`. Rust's `staticlib` crate-type output naming is platform-specific -- confirmed empirically by cross-building `pycc_rt` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` locally and inspecting the result directly: `target/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/debug/pycc_rt.lib`, not `libpycc_rt.a` -- no `lib` prefix, `.lib` extension, COFF format.
  3. Even after fixing (1) and (2) plus adding the Windows system-libs list below, a bare `clang.exe` invocation with no explicit `-target` flag resolved inconsistently across otherwise byte-identical command lines in the same job: some invocations correctly selected MSVC's `lld-link`, others silently fell back to a MinGW/GCC toolchain discovered on `PATH` (`C:\mingw64`) -- which cannot link `pycc_rt.lib`'s MSVC-ABI symbols (`__imp_closesocket`, `__chkstk`, the MSVC RTTI vtable), producing a wall of GNU `ld`/`collect2` "undefined reference" errors instead of a normal MSVC link. Confirmed directly via clang's own `-v` verbose output, which showed "Target: x86_64-pc-windows-msvc" and a correct, complete `lld-link` invocation for every test once `-target` was forced explicitly.
  4. Separately, `pycc_codegen/src/lib.rs`'s own test-only `link_object_with_runtime` helper (added so that crate's tests can prove its object-file output actually links and runs, without a dependency cycle on the `pycc` binary crate) duplicated a minimal linking step and *also* hardcoded `Command::new("cc")`, with none of (1)-(3)'s fixes applied -- a second, independent call site the first round of fixes never touched, which is exactly why 4 tests kept failing with (1)'s original symptom one full CI round after `src/main.rs` had already been fixed.
  5. Even with (1) fixed, `clang.exe`'s bundled `lld-link` initially failed with `cannot open libcmt.lib`/`oldnames.lib`: `rustc`'s own link step auto-discovers the MSVC toolset (`LIB`/`INCLUDE`/CRT search paths) internally, but a direct `clang.exe` invocation outside any MSVC Developer Command Prompt does not.
- Decision: `#[cfg(windows)]`/`#[cfg(not(windows))]`-gated (compile-time conditional compilation, not a `cfg!()` runtime check, for the same D-014 coverage-gate reason as everywhere else in this arc) at both call sites:
  - The linker binary: bundled `<LLVM_SYS_221_PREFIX>/bin/clang.exe` on Windows, `cc` elsewhere -- the same official LLVM release D-027 already requires, not a new dependency.
  - Both call sites now always pass an explicit `-target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` on Windows rather than relying on clang's bare-invocation default, since (3) showed that default is not reliable.
  - The expected static-lib filename is keyed on the *requested* `--target` triple (`pycc_rt.lib` for an `-msvc` triple, `libpycc_rt.a` otherwise), not a host `#[cfg(windows)]` constant -- the first version used a host-keyed constant, which silently checked for the wrong filename whenever `--target` crossed OS families; caught and fixed in PR review before merge (D-031).
  - Both call sites pass the same explicit Windows system-libs list (`ws2_32`, `ntdll`, `userenv`, `advapi32`, `shell32`, `ole32`, `uuid`, `psapi`, `dbghelp`, `kernel32`, `legacy_stdio_definitions`), sourced from rustc's own captured link-invocation log for `pycc.exe` on the same CI runner, not guessed -- needed because linking directly via a compiler driver, rather than through `cargo`/`rustc`'s own link step, doesn't get Rust std's transitively-required Windows libraries for free.
  - For (5): the `native-build-test` job sets up an MSVC Developer Command Prompt environment (`ilammy/msvc-dev-cmd@v1`, the standard action for this) before the LLVM install steps, giving the bundled `clang.exe`/`lld-link` the same `LIB`/`INCLUDE` environment `vcvarsall.bat` would.
- Alternatives: hand-roll a direct `lld-link`/`ld.lld`/`ld64.lld` invocation on every platform, matching ARCHITECTURE.md's literal "LLVM IR → object code → lld → native binary" diagram and D-011's "bundled lld" wording exactly -- rejected for *this* PR: it would mean reconstructing, per OS, all the default libraries/CRT-startup-object/entry-point arguments a compiler driver normally supplies for free (verified none of the three LLVM distributions this project already installs actually bundle an `lld`/`ld64.lld`/`ld.lld` *binary* -- Homebrew's `llvm` formula ships `lldb`, the debugger, not the linker), which is a substantially bigger undertaking than the rest of PR-3 combined. Sharing one linking helper between `pycc` (the binary crate) and `pycc_codegen`'s own tests, to avoid (4)'s duplication -- rejected, same as when `link_object_with_runtime` was first written: it would invert the crate dependency graph (`pycc` depends on `pycc_codegen`, not the reverse). Given that constraint, duplicating the now four-part Windows-specific linking logic a second time is an accepted cost, not an oversight -- but it is exactly why the second call site was missed for a full CI round, since nothing enforces the two copies stay in sync.
- Consequences: `pycc`'s own linking step still depends on a driver-plus-toolchain (system `cc` on macOS/Linux, bundled `clang` calling system `link.exe` on Windows) rather than the fully self-contained "bundled lld, no system toolchain required" end state D-011/ARCHITECTURE.md describe -- a real, tracked gap (same honesty pattern as D-026's cross-OS scoping), not silently declared done. Revisit when a PR specifically targets toolchain-independent linking. Additionally: any future change to Windows linking arguments (system libs, target, toolchain path) must be applied at both `src/main.rs`'s `try_build` and `pycc_codegen/src/lib.rs`'s `link_object_with_runtime` -- nothing currently enforces this stays in sync, a known follow-up risk worth revisiting if a third call site is ever added.

## D-029: LLVM's `LLVMDisposeMessage` crashes on Windows for the official prebuilt 22.1.1 release

- Status: accepted (PR-3 is the PR that depends on it)
- Context: with D-028's first two fixes in place, `native-build-test (windows-latest)` still failed `cargo test --workspace` -- the same integration tests, with the same signature: the spawned `pycc.exe` exits with code `-1073741819` (`0xC0000005`, `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` -- a genuine native crash, not a clean error or a Rust panic). Localized by instrumenting `compile_to_object` with unbuffered `eprintln!` checkpoints bracketing every call from the end of IR building through object emission, then reading the actual CI log: every failing run printed the checkpoint immediately before `module.verify()` and never the one immediately after. Fixing that (see Decision below) eliminated the crash for every test *except* `an_unknown_target_triple_is_a_clean_build_error`, which still crashed identically -- revealing this was never really "one bug at `verify()`," but one underlying defect (`inkwell::support::LLVMString`'s `Drop` impl calls `LLVMDisposeMessage`, which faults on this platform/LLVM-release combination) surfacing at whichever call happened to be the first to construct and drop an `LLVMString` on a given code path:
  1. `module.verify()` itself doesn't return an `LLVMString`, but every test that reaches it also constructs a `TargetTriple` (whose `triple` field is an owned `LLVMString`) shortly afterward; checkpoint evidence pointed at `verify()` as the crash site specifically, since it consistently ran last among printed checkpoints.
  2. `TargetTriple::create`/`TargetMachine::get_default_triple`'s returned `TargetTriple` owns an `LLVMString` whose `Drop` runs when the triple goes out of scope at the end of `compile_to_object` -- still crashed even with `verify()` skipped, since skipping `verify()` fixed its own call site but not the real underlying `Drop` defect.
  3. `Target::from_triple`'s `Err` branch separately returns its own `LLVMString` (`Err(LLVMString::new(...))`), and `TargetMachine::write_to_file`'s `Err` is likewise `LLVMString` -- two more values of the exact same type crossing into this crate's error paths, each hit only by a test that actually exercises that specific error branch (`an_unknown_target_triple_is_a_clean_build_error`, specifically, for (3)'s first half).
- Decision: a `#[cfg(windows)]`/`#[cfg(not(windows))]`-gated `verify_module()` helper (no-op on Windows; the original `.expect()`-wrapped `verify()` call elsewhere) for (1) -- a targeted skip of a *pure internal sanity check*, not a change to what gets compiled: the call's own message already says a failure here means a `pycc_codegen` bug, never a rejection of legitimate user code. `std::mem::ManuallyDrop::new(...)` at the point `TargetTriple` is created, covering every exit path including early `?` returns, for (2). A general `llvm_string_to_owned(message: LLVMString) -> String` helper (`.to_string()` then `std::mem::forget()`, rather than patching each call site's error-conversion by hand) applied at both `Target::from_triple`'s and `write_to_file`'s `.map_err(...)`, for (3) -- chosen over fixing each call site individually specifically so a *future* `LLVMString`-typed value crossing into this crate's error paths doesn't reintroduce the same crash silently.
- Alternatives: root-causing the crash inside LLVM's own compiled `LLVMDisposeMessage` -- rejected as open-ended with no reachable next step from this environment (no Windows debugger available to get an exact fault address). An alternate verification API for (1) specifically (per-function `LLVMVerifyFunction`, or a raw non-inkwell `llvm-sys` call bypassing inkwell's wrapper) -- rejected once (2) and (3) showed the *real* defect was `Drop`/`LLVMDisposeMessage`, not `verify()`'s own logic, making an alternate verify API beside the point.
- Consequences: Windows CI no longer gets `pycc_codegen`'s own well-formed-IR self-check from (1) -- a real, narrow, tracked gap. The compensating fact: IR-building has zero platform-conditional logic anywhere in this crate, and the identical code stays verified on macOS/Linux every commit, so a bug that only produces malformed IR on Windows would require Windows-specific codegen logic that doesn't exist yet. Any future `LLVMString`-returning API this crate starts calling must route its error conversion through `llvm_string_to_owned` (or wrap owned values in `ManuallyDrop`, if kept alive rather than immediately converted) on Windows, or risk reintroducing this exact crash silently -- revisit if a future LLVM/inkwell version fixes the underlying `LLVMDisposeMessage` defect, at which point all three workarounds could potentially be reverted together.

## D-030: CI steps must not force `shell: bash` on Windows without a reason

- Status: accepted (PR-3 is the PR that depends on it)
